### The phases

#### Phase 1. Batch 5: what is decided and shares nothing

- The wrapping operators ∔ ∸ ⨰ declared in the interpreter's library. The library bodies and the five team tests that rely on wrapping are rewritten with them. No output changes (inventory B1 step 13; POSITIONS 2026-09-26, rung O).
- Rung S: the specification revised for route A, except the number chapters. Everything it describes already runs on both paths (inventory B1 step 10; POSITIONS 2026-09-26, S1, S2 and the number chapters).
- The run-time size rung (design B): a sized program compiles, loads and dispatches on its size. Row 402's size case in the exclusion rule can land at its gather (inventory B1 step 12; POSITIONS 2026-09-24, design B).

Needs answers 1 to 5 below.

#### Phase 2. Batch 6: the tower flips, the specification with it

- The flattening rung. `ZZ`, `QQ` and `RR64` become siblings under `Number`, a wider type converts from a narrower one by `coerce`, and `SUM`'s catch-all is replaced (inventory B1 step 9; POSITIONS 2026-09-24, route A).
- The three number chapters of the specification, in the same batch (POSITIONS 2026-09-26, the number chapters).
- Rung O: the ten natives raise `IntegerOverflow`, with the count of changed tests measured again and expected at zero (inventory B1 step 13).

Needs answers 6 to 8. By inference, the checker count after it is about 44, not the 22 on record, because rung N uncovered 22 `fill` errors the flattening does not touch (inventory B2, C10).

34. The order of the compiled checker's attempts at a call, and the declaration it chooses under an expected type (row 508). Rung I tries a call by subtyping with the expected type, then with coercion and the expected type, and only then without it (the shadow's order, accepted by its first skeptic and judge). So `a: V = k(NOf(1))`, with `k[\T extends N\](x: T): T` beside `k(x: V): V`, runs the converted `k(V)` where the base and walk run the generic that fits, against decision 1 ("a conversion ... never changes which declaration runs when one already fits") and the coercion chapter's Σ, taken on the arguments (`Specification/basic/conversions-coercions.tex:529-537`). At a method invocation the base already does the same. It landed as a reserved stop, gated by the expected failure `ProjectFortress/compiler_tests/XXXInferContextKeepsFit`. The alternative: try subtyping without the context, kept when its head's result converts, before coercion with the context; its cost is re-measuring every call whose instance the context now fixes (`c: ZZ64 = idt(3)` would run at `IntLiteral`, the binding converting). Rung T's skeptic measured the same mechanism on the base at an operator and a method invocation (`d: ZZ64 = z OPLUS z` runs `OPLUS(ZZ64, ZZ32)` with `z` converted where the generic fits; `O.k(z)` likewise), and the new inference chapter states decision 1's order (`Specification/basic/inference.tex:60-69`, `:120-126`); on the merged tree the call written `k(z)` runs `k(ZZ64)` too. The two faces are gated as `ProjectFortress/compiler_tests/XXXExpectedTypeChoiceRungT` and `XXXExpectedTypeFnChoiceRungT`. The combined review of batch N's first run found that the checker's landed order runs against decision 1 and against the landed chapter (`reviews/batch-N-review.md`, finding 2). The chapter already composes decisions 1 and 3: the declaration is chosen as the coercion chapter says, and a coercion "never changes which declaration is chosen when one is applicable to the call without it" (`inference.tex:60-69`); the expected type then fixes the chosen declaration's instance, with the retry without it (`:118-126`). Decided 2026-09-29 at 15:49 UTC, R4's option 1 (POSITIONS 2026-09-29, the order of the compiled checker's attempts at a call): the chapter's order, which agrees with decision 1, built as the alternative above, subtyping without the context, kept when its head's result converts, before coercion with the context, in one checker rung of batch 8 with `XXXInferContextKeepsFit` promoted (phase 3, batch 8's line); its cost is re-measuring the calls whose instance the context fixes. Until it is built, the checker runs the landed order, gated as above. Evidence: `compile-ladder/rung-inference-checker/SKEPTIC.md:56` (section 4), `compile-ladder/rung-inference-checker/REPORT.md:494` (section 9), `compile-ladder/rung-inference-checker/probes/skeptic/S2CtxConvWinsW.diff.txt:6`, `:14`, `:19`; `compile-ladder/rung-spec-inference/SKEPTIC.md:261`, the first judgement's section 3, its F5; `compile-ladder/climb-batch-N/merged-tests/both-SkK17-SkExpRun.txt`. The one library's tuple comparisons and `LexicographicOrder` compare values of an unbounded type parameter (row 634, 18 sites of the distance): a bound such as `StandardPartialOrder` refuses what walk compares today, a pair of pairs among them, and `List[\E\]` extends `LexicographicOrder` at every element type; the text compares lists whose elements support the comparison and is silent on tuples. Built at the judgement's default, way 1b (`reviews/tuple-comparisons-judgement.md`; `coordinator/CLIMB-BATCH-13.md`, Q43), on his standing go for phase 3, by climb batch 13's rung O while his answer is pending: each element of the ten tuple operators bounded by `StandardPartialOrder` (`Library/FortressLibrary.fss:4476-4585`, `.fsi:2633-2643`), each `typecase` answering `Unordered => false`, and `Comparison`'s lazy `LEXICO`; the 17 tuple sites are gone and `LexicographicOrder`'s stays (row 634's list half). Two costs of the default that the judgement did not weigh, from the rung and its skeptic. The judgement read a pair with `()`, a function or an unordered object as an element as 'a stop either way' (`reviews/tuple-comparisons-judgement.md:82`), but on the base such a pair answered wherever an earlier element decided, `(1,()) < (2,())` being `true`, so the bound turns answers into refusals at the call (`ProjectFortress/tests/TupleOrderUnitRefused.test:1`). And walk refuses a pair whose elements at one position are numbers of two run-time types: `(1,2) < (1,2.5)`, which answered `true`, and `(1,2) < (widen(1),2)`, which answered `false`, since walk takes the element type at their join, which is no `StandardPartialOrder`, without promoting the position first (row 511's tuple shape; `ProjectFortress/tests/TupleOrderMixedRefused.test:1`); the judgement's list of unchanged values (`reviews/tuple-comparisons-judgement.md:83`) does not name such a pair. The ways for the second: keep the refusal, the default as built; have walk promote the position first, a walk edit under row 511; or bound the operators otherwise. Default: as built. Evidence: `compile-ladder/rung-tuple-orders/REPORT.md:295` (decision 3), `SKEPTIC.md:300` (section 10).

- The skeptic's contested rule, its third clause, from climb batch 11's review (`reviews/batch-11-review.md`, Part 2, "The contested fix, and whether another should have been", finding 2 and measure 1). A skeptic's fix is contested when "it touches a path the section does not give the rung, or reaches a point to report" (`coordinator/climb-batch-workflow.js:807`, clause (c); the `coordinator` skill's `references/batch.md`, "The roles"). Rung C's skeptic marked its fix `15a4be724` contested only because it reached the point "A library or walk edit", which the worker's own row 626 edit had already reached; the judge upheld it, 138K tokens and 3.4 minutes for a ruling that changed nothing. Rung E's skeptic, whose fix `2579d7e5b` a cited sentence settled and clause (a) could have contested, let the sentence win; rung C's let the clause win; the rule does not say which wins. The measure: a fix is contested for a point to report only when the worker's change did not already reach that point, and a fix that a cited sentence settles is settled unless the worker's report cites a source for the behaviour it changes. It saves about 0.14M a ruling like rung C's. Answered after climb batch 13's review, option 1 ("Option 1, narrow the rule"): a fix the skeptic shows settled by a cited sentence is not contested for reaching a point to report or a path outside the section; it lands and the point or path goes in `pointsReached` for him (the `coordinator` skill's `references/batch.md`, "The roles"; the skeptic's clause (c) in `coordinator/climb-batch-workflow.js` and its manual, `a1902d0d4`). Built for batch 14.

#### Climb batch 12, listed for his review

- Do a `try` block and its `catch` clauses, and the body of an `atomic` or `tryatomic` expression, take the expected type of the whole, by the reading taken for a `typecase` clause (climb batch 11's Q2) and a `label` body (Q48(a))? Climb batch 12's rung C, carried by its skeptic: `try.tex` gives a `try` the union of the type of its block and those of its `catch` clauses' right-hand sides, the label's form (`Specification/basic/expressions/try.tex:103-105`), and `atomic.tex` gives an `atomic` the value and type of its body, a block's form (`Specification/basic/expressions/atomic.tex:42-43`). The compiled checker gives them no expected type (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/scala_src/typechecker/impls/Misc.scala:782`, `:801`, `:451-458`) and joins the `finally` block's type into the `try`'s type (`:816-820`), which `try.tex:103-105` does not. Not measured. Yes: a checker rung measures and repairs them, and the inference chapter's list gains them; no: the "not yet described" item (`Specification/basic/inference.tex:277-278`) names them. Default: as landed, the second: the checker gives them no expected type and the chapter's "not yet described" item names them (the `coordinator` skill's `SKILL.md`, "The curator's role and decisions"; `reviews/batch-12-review.md`, Part 4). Evidence: `compile-ladder/rung-checker-contexts/REPORT.md:189` (section 10).
- Should the inference chapter's list of contexts (`Specification/basic/inference.tex:128-148`) gain an expression that is not the last element of a block, with `()`, the type every such expression must have (`Specification/basic/expressions/blocks.tex:49-50`)? Climb batch 12's rung C's skeptic: the compiled checker has always checked a block's non-last elements against `()` (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/scala_src/typechecker/impls/Misc.scala:433-434`), so `do stop("s"); 3 end` checks on the base; with row 642's repair a label in that position passes `()` to its exits' `with` values, refused on `7fa767d48` and accepted since the rung. The list does not name the context, and its "not yet described" item (`:277-278`) covers it. Yes: the list gains it, by the reading rung E applied to a clause of an `if` without `else`, and Appendix I's entry "The contexts that give a call an expected type" records why; no: the item names it and the checker's practice stays undescribed. Default: as landed, the list unchanged. Evidence: `compile-ladder/rung-checker-contexts/SKEPTIC.md:151` (section 2.6).
- A program the text may allow is now refused where the checker crashed, from the skeptic's fix `b73143297` that climb batch 12's rung C's judge upheld: `k(): Any = atomic do g = fn (): Any => spawn 3; g end` crashed on the base and on the worker's head, 'Not in the trait table: CompilerBuiltin.Thread', and is now refused, "A 'spawn' expression must not occur inside an 'atomic' do block.". The record does not settle whether the atomic body's ban on `spawn` is read by where the code is written or by when it runs: a `spawn` "cannot be run within the body of an `atomic` expression" (`Specification/basic/expressions/spawn.tex:28-31`), and static checking of `io` actions is not supported (`:15`). The team's checker reads it by where the code is written: `AtomicChecker`'s constructor makes every `extend` an `AtomicChecker` (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/scala_src/typechecker/impls/Misc.scala:971-979`), and an untyped function expression's body is checked through `extend` (`impls/Functionals.scala:1152`). Way (a), taken: keep that reading for a typed function expression too, so such a program is refused. Way (b): `SFnExpr` (`impls/Functionals.scala:1128-1160`) checks its body with a plain checker, a change to the team's checker by which no program gains a run, since the code generator has no `spawn` visitor (FACTS, "The territory map"). `XXXSpawnInAtomicExitValue` stays under either way. Default: (a), as landed. Evidence: `compile-ladder/rung-checker-contexts/JUDGE.md:55` (section 7), `SKEPTIC.md:183` (section 4).

- QW-c, from climb batch 13's rung W's skeptic and judge: what does an `extends` clause's own `where` clause mean, as in `trait G[\T\] extends A[\T\] where { T extends ZZ32 }` with `override f(x: ZZ32)` over `A[\T\]`'s `f(x: T)`? The specification gives only the grammar of such a clause (`Specification/basic/traits.tex:76-83`; the extends prose at `:156-198` is silent), and walk reads the extension as unconditional (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/interpreter/evaluator/BuildEnvironments.java:1070`). Way (a), built as a lenience in the skeptic's `e8d377669`, upheld (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/interpreter/evaluator/values/Constructor.java:634-666`): the inherited declarations are read under the clause, so the override overrides, gated by `ProjectFortress/tests/OverrideConditionalExtensionWalk.fss`. Way (b): the extension is read unconditionally and the override is a static error, as on the worker's head `6bfd27720`. The answer belongs with the language's reading of conditional inheritance, which no implementation here models. Default: (a), as landed. Evidence: `compile-ladder/rung-walk-override/SKEPTIC.md:202` (section 5), `JUDGE.md:149` (section 5).

- gather.2 of climb batch 20's gather: the dimensions chapter writes its unit factors with an integer numeral base at a negative exponent, `inch` as `2.54 \times 10^{-2}` meter, its conversion of miles as `((2.54 \times 10^{-2})(12)(5280)/10^{3})`, "or `25146/15625`", an exact rational, and the SI prefixes `deci` to `yocto` as `10^{-1}` to `10^{-24}` (`Specification/advanced/defining-dimensions.tex:217`, `:224-225`, `:310-319`). ### Phase 4. The switch-over

- **Climb batch 23** (C and W of `coordinator/CLIMB-BATCH-23.md`, phase 4's first batch, the natives' mechanism, N0 in the natives line below, with N1's first family folded into rung W), landed 2026-10-30 at `df71bc585` (rung C `98b04d371`, rung W `640acb4a1`; `compile-ladder/climb-batch-23/RECORD.md`; its record reviewed before the launch, `coordinator/climb-batch-23-review.md`, and re-checked on climb batch 22c's landing, `c1a7c7eb0`; review `reviews/batch-23-review.md`): the count 0 to 0 and the distance 0 to 0 over the fourteen, '#crash none', now with 21 bindings read as calls of their helpers (F-53, F-298, F-299); rows 309 and 796 closed, row 336 rewritten, rows 845 to 849 opened; the 52 team demos run to their end on the merged tree; the count stage about 155 s and the distance stage 240 s at the gate (F-240); its questions 1 and 2 taken at their defaults with his go of 22:36:37 UTC on 2026-10-29; its items for Pavol under "Climb batch 23, listed for his review".
- The natives (`perf-probes/prelude/natives-shape.md`, R5), first in the phase by P-33's order, and the third of the three steps that `perf-probes/prelude/REPORT.md` named (F-61). The compiled path cannot reuse walk's glue classes, the Java classes that most bindings name (F-32). The one library has 449 live native bindings, sorted at `a1f8c4b8b`, before any moved (F-32): 154 the compiled prelude already binds at the same owner and signature, 59 with a helper nothing binds yet, 158 that need a new static helper, 78 other (36 methods of native I/O objects, 42 with no compiled analogue); the 24 `IntLiteral` bindings among them wait for N5 (P-76; row 578). The one binding text for both paths, item 35's answer, is `builtinPrimitive` naming a static helper (P-58, "Every native binding is a `builtinPrimitive` call naming a static helper."). N0, the mechanism, is built (climb batch 23, phase 4's first line): the compiled path rewrites such a body at the parse into a call of the helper, on its own repository and in the gate's two stages, never under walk, from N0 on, where this line had the compile command switch it on from N1 (F-298; fork 2), and walk loads the helper beside its glue classes (F-299). With it N1's first family moved, 21 bindings, 19 of `ZZ32`'s and `Int`'s `asString` and `asExprString`, so 428 still name glue classes (F-32). The plan, sized on the earlier count of 424 (`natives-shape.md` section 3): nine rungs, about three gated batches, before the switch-over and beside phase 3 (P-33), since they touch walk and the library's binding bodies and not the checker, moving walk onto the helpers one family at a time, each gated by the interpreter suite: N0, the mechanism (1 rung, Java), built; N1, the 147 bound natives (2, library text only), its first family built; N2, the 61 with a helper, the 12 wrapping operators bound to the wrapping helpers (1); N3, the 132 new helpers and their 138 bindings (3, Java and library); N4, the I/O objects, a Java-backed writer type and `stdOut` first, since every program's `println` goes through `Writer` (2). N1's next families, for climb batch 24's planner (`coordinator/CLIMB-BATCH-23.md`, section 4, "The planner's probes" and "Left out, each with its home"; `reviews/batch-23-review.md`, finding 6): of the 104 bindings that can name their helper as a bare string, 83 are left; the 109 that cannot need a way first, `Char`'s 40, `NN32`'s 22 and `NN64`'s 20, whose helpers' `int` and `long` the stand-in table reads as `ZZ32` and `ZZ64` (helpers with the special names, the table reading a helper's types from its binding's declaration, or a second declaration that converts, which P-58's bare form cannot write in one body; not measured), and the others over `AnyIntegral`, `Number` or `Any`, whose prelude form composes two helpers, or that answer `Float`; `ZZ32`'s `CHOOSE` moves with row 521's helper fix (below); `Int`'s `asFloat`, and the `asFloat`s of `RR32` and `NN32`, with the declared result at `RR64` or a helper answering walk's type, since `Float` is narrower than the helper's `RR64` and the checker refuses the move (`ProjectFortress/LibraryBuiltin/FortressBuiltin.fss:375`; fork 12); row 336's rest, `REM` and `MOD` by zero (new helpers, N3) and `ZZ64`'s and `ZZ`'s `DIV` (their families' rungs), with row 848, its siblings on `NN32`, `NN64` and `ZZ`, each on its helper or by a zero test; a binding over `String` after his answer to item 62 (row 846); and row 847, a helper string in a method of a type that no Java type carries, which loads under walk, a walk rung's fix that tells the loader the declaring type and refuses such a binding at load. On the switch-over day, N5 inside the names batch: the foreign-type table, the helpers' 55 raise strings, the `IntLiteral` helpers and the source files that name the prelude. ### Phase 6. microGPT fast

- Unboxed arithmetic chosen by static type in generated code (decision B), which P-17 requires. On array code boxing costs 6.3 to 6.5 times (inventory B1 step 18; FACTS § Execution model).
- The transaction checks around mutable variables (row 641): the compiled code checks for a transaction before every read and write of a mutable variable, outside `atomic` too, about 1 to 2 ns a check, 64 % of a loop over an array with mutable locals and 9 to 15 % of a scalar accumulator loop (`explorations/perf-probes/transactions/REPORT.md`). What the specification's `atomic` requires of a variable no transaction can reach, and how the code generator leaves those out, is to be understood first; the curator wants the transactional memory's role in the design described before it is changed.
